Used by: [`terracotta-rainscreen`](./terracotta-rainscreen/SKILL.md).

- Extrude the panel hollow in a die, with a wall of 15 to 30 mm.
- Fire the panel once, with an engobe or a glaze if the design needs it.
- Hang each panel on a clip and a rail of stainless steel.
- Keep an air gap of 20 to 50 mm and an open joint of 6 to 10 mm.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Rainscreen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rainscreen); [Wikipedia: Architectural terracotta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architectural_terracotta).

### Ceramming

A formed piece of glass is heated in two steps. Fine crystals grow in the glass and make it a glass-ceramic that does not break in a heat shock.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | 1953 | — | Corning, New York: S. Donald Stookey finds that a glass left in a furnace at too high a heat does not melt and turns opaque. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | 1958 | +5 years | Corning turns the process into cookware: pans of white glass-ceramic go from the freezer to a gas flame. | [`glass-ceramic-cookware`](./glass-ceramic-cookware/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`glass-ceramic-cookware`](./glass-ceramic-cookware/SKILL.md).

- Melt a glass of lithium, aluminium and silica with a nucleating agent such as titanium dioxide.
- Form the piece as glass and cool it.
- Hold the piece at about 750 °C, so that nuclei form.
- Raise the heat to about 1100 °C, so that the crystals grow and fill the glass.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Glass-ceramic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glass-ceramic); [Wikipedia: S. Donald Stookey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S._Donald_Stookey).

### Ceviche curing

Raw fish stays in lime juice with salt and chilli for a few minutes, until the surface turns firm and opaque, and is served at once with its juice.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Cuisine | c. 1820 | — | Lima, Peru: The first firm records of the lime cure of fish are of the early nineteenth century in Lima. | |
| 1 | Cuisine | 1994 | +174 years | Gastón Acurio served ceviche as a fine-dining course at Astrid y Gastón in Lima. | [`novoandina-and-nikkei`](./novoandina-and-nikkei/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`novoandina-and-nikkei`](./novoandina-and-nikkei/SKILL.md).

- Cut very fresh fish into cubes of 2 cm and chill it to 4 °C.
- Add salt, chilli and the juice of fresh limes in the same bowl.
- Leave the fish in the acid for 2 to 4 minutes, no more.
- Serve at once in a cold bowl with the juice and a sweet or crisp side.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Ceviche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceviche); [Wikipedia: Gastón Acurio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gast%C3%B3n_Acurio).

### Chiaroscuro

A hard contrast of light and dark that models form: one strong source, deep shadow and a small area of light.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Painting | c. 1500 | — | Italy: Italian painters model form with strong light and dark from the late 15th century; Caravaggio makes the contrast extreme in The Calling of Saint Matthew (1599–1600). | |
| 1 | Painting | c. 1600 | +100 years | Caravaggio paints figures out of a dark ground with one hard shaft of light, and the Baroque painters follow. | [`baroque-painting`](./baroque-painting/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Painting | 1766 | +266 years | Joseph Wright of Derby, and the Romantic painters after him, light night scenes from one candle, fire or moon. | [`romanticism-painting`](./romanticism-painting/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Film & Video | 1915 | +415 years | Cecil B. DeMille and the cameraman Alvin Wyckoff light The Cheat with hard side light and call the result Rembrandt lighting. |  |
| 4 | Film & Video | 1920 | +420 years | The Cabinet of Dr. Caligari uses painted shadows and sharp light to set a mind in a distorted room. | [`german-expressionist-cinema`](./german-expressionist-cinema/SKILL.md) |
| 5 | Film & Video | 1941 | +441 years | Citizen Kane and The Maltese Falcon use low-key light, deep shadow and hard bars of light; crime films of the 1940s make it a house style. | [`film-noir`](./film-noir/SKILL.md) |
| 6 | Photography | c. 1946 | +446 years | Stills of noir films and press night photographs keep the single hard light and the black ground. | [`film-noir-still`](./film-noir-still/SKILL.md) |
| 7 | Film & Video | 1982 | +482 years | Blade Runner, shot by Jordan Cronenweth for Ridley Scott, cuts shafts of light through smoke and blinds in a colour city. | [`neon-noir`](./neon-noir/SKILL.md) |
| 8 | Internet | c. 2019 | +519 years | Dark academia photographs light a face or a book with one candle or window and let the rest fall into shadow. | [`dark-academia`](./dark-academia/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`baroque-painting`](./baroque-painting/SKILL.md), [`german-expressionist-cinema`](./german-expressionist-cinema/SKILL.md), [`film-noir`](./film-noir/SKILL.md), [`film-noir-still`](./film-noir-still/SKILL.md), [`neon-noir`](./neon-noir/SKILL.md), [`romanticism-painting`](./romanticism-painting/SKILL.md), [`dark-academia`](./dark-academia/SKILL.md).

- Light the subject from one hard source at 30° to 60° above and to the side; let the shadow side fall to near black.
- Keep the light on 20% to 30% of the frame and let the rest sink into shadow.
- Set the key light to fill light at a ratio of 8:1 or more for a dramatic mood.
- Cut hard shapes into the light with blinds, grilles or foliage so that the shadow becomes a pattern.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Chiaroscuro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiaroscuro); David Bordwell and Kristin Thompson, Film Art: An Introduction (McGraw-Hill).

### Chroma key

Remove one flat colour from a shot and put another picture behind the subject.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Film & Video | 1940 | — | London: Larry Butler makes the blue-screen travelling-matte shots of The Thief of Bagdad. | |
| 1 | Internet | 2020 | +80 years | TikTok adds a green-screen effect: the creator keeps the face and body and swaps the ground for any photo. | [`green-screen-short`](./green-screen-short/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`green-screen-short`](./green-screen-short/SKILL.md).

- Light a flat green or blue ground evenly, with no shadows.
- Keep the subject at 1 m or more from the ground to avoid colour spill.
- Key out the ground colour and feather the mask edge by 1px to 2px.
- Place the new picture behind the subject and match its light direction.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Chroma key](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chroma_key); [Wikipedia: Lawrence W. Butler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence_W._Butler).

### Chronophotography

A series of photographs, or many exposures on one plate, that split a movement into steps and lay the steps side by side.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Photography | 1878 | — | Palo Alto: Eadweard Muybridge records a galloping horse with a row of cameras; Étienne-Jules Marey takes many exposures on one plate from 1882. | |
| 1 | Photography | 1882 | +4 years | Étienne-Jules Marey makes his chronophotographic gun and fixed plate in Paris. |  |
| 2 | Photography | 1911 | +33 years | Anton Giulio Bragaglia publishes photodynamism, a blurred photograph of movement. |  |
| 3 | Painting | 1912 | +34 years | Giacomo Balla paints Dynamism of a Dog on a Leash with repeated legs and tail. | [`futurism-painting`](./futurism-painting/SKILL.md) |
| 4 | Painting | 1912 | +34 years | Marcel Duchamp paints Nude Descending a Staircase No. 2 in stages. | [`cubism-painting`](./cubism-painting/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`futurism-painting`](./futurism-painting/SKILL.md), [`cubism-painting`](./cubism-painting/SKILL.md).

- Record 12 to 24 frames each second, or 5 to 20 exposures on one plate.
- Hold the camera and the dark ground still; the body moves against a plain field.
- Show each stage of the movement in a form that overlaps its neighbour by 20% to 50%.
- In a painting, repeat a limb or a form along the path of the movement.

Sources: Marta Braun, Picturing Time: The Work of Étienne-Jules Marey (University of Chicago Press, 1992); Giacomo Balla, Dynamism of a Dog on a Leash (1912).

### Copper rivet reinforcement

A line of grease and manganese oxide is drawn round each colour field on an unfired tile, so that the coloured glazes do not run into each other. The line fires to a matt dark edge.

| Origin | Internet | 2019 | — | Online (Dribbble): Designers on Dribbble post soft-plastic interface concepts, and the name neumorphism spreads. | |

Used by: [`neumorphism`](./neumorphism/SKILL.md), [`claymorphism`](./claymorphism/SKILL.md).

- Use the ground colour for the shape; no border.
- Cast a dark shadow 6 to 12 px to the bottom right and a light shadow at the same distance to the top left.
- Blur each shadow by 2 times its distance.

Sources: Michal Malewicz, Neumorphism in user interfaces (2019).

### Dust pressing

Nearly dry clay powder is pressed in a steel die under high pressure to form a tile in one step. The tile needs little drying and keeps its size.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | 1840 | — | Staffordshire, England: Richard Prosser takes out a patent for pressing tiles from clay dust, and Minton uses it for floor tiles. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | 1840 | +0 years | Minton and other Staffordshire firms press floor tiles from dust in a steel die, and the tiles have square, even edges. | [`victorian-encaustic-tiles`](./victorian-encaustic-tiles/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Ceramics | 1904 | +64 years | New York tile works press small white wall tiles from dust, with bevelled edges and ribs on the back. | [`subway-tile`](./subway-tile/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`victorian-encaustic-tiles`](./victorian-encaustic-tiles/SKILL.md), [`subway-tile`](./subway-tile/SKILL.md).

- Grind the clay and sieve it to a fine powder with about 6 per cent water.
- Fill the steel die and press the powder under a screw or hydraulic press.
- Remove the tile and fire it with no long drying.
- Keep the size of each tile within 0.5 mm.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Tile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tile); [Wikipedia: Minton tiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minton_%26_Co.).

### Electroplating

An electric current puts a thin layer of one metal on a part of another metal. Silver, gold, nickel and chromium plate give a cheap base metal a precious or hard surface.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Industrial design | 1840 | — | Birmingham, United Kingdom: George and Henry Elkington patent cyanide silver plating; Luigi Brugnatelli gilded silver by electrolysis in 1805. | |
| 1 | Industrial design | 1840 | +0 years | Elkington silver plate replaces Sheffield plate on factory-made tableware. | [`victorian-manufacture`](./victorian-manufacture/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Fashion | c. 1924 | +84 years | Gilt and chrome plate give cast base-metal jewels the look of gold and platinum. | [`costume-jewellery`](./costume-jewellery/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Fashion | c. 1925 | +85 years | Nickel and chrome plate cover the lamps, the radiator and the bumpers of the car. | [`vintage-coachbuilt-car`](./vintage-coachbuilt-car/SKILL.md) |
| 4 | Industrial design | 1925 | +85 years | Nickel-plated brass and chrome-plated steel become the metal of the modern lamp and chair. | [`art-deco-product`](./art-deco-product/SKILL.md), [`bauhaus-product`](./bauhaus-product/SKILL.md) |
| 5 | Industrial design | c. 1950 | +110 years | Silver plate and chrome stay on Nordic tableware and lamps next to stainless steel. | [`scandinavian-modern-product`](./scandinavian-modern-product/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`victorian-manufacture`](./victorian-manufacture/SKILL.md), [`costume-jewellery`](./costume-jewellery/SKILL.md), [`vintage-coachbuilt-car`](./vintage-coachbuilt-car/SKILL.md), [`art-deco-product`](./art-deco-product/SKILL.md), [`bauhaus-product`](./bauhaus-product/SKILL.md), [`scandinavian-modern-product`](./scandinavian-modern-product/SKILL.md).

- Clean and degrease the base part, then put it in a bath of a salt of the coating metal.
- Connect the part to the negative pole and a bar of the coating metal to the positive pole.
- Pass a direct current until the layer is 0.5 to 25 µm thick.
- Put a copper or nickel under-layer on steel and zinc before a bright top layer.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Electroplating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electroplating); [Wikipedia: Elkington & Co.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elkington_%26_Co.).

### Etching

An intaglio print from a metal plate whose lines a needle draws through an acid-resistant ground and an acid then bites.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Print | 1513 | — | Basel: Urs Graf makes the first dated etching, on an iron plate. Daniel Hopfer etched prints from about 1500, and armourers used acid to decorate metal before that. | |
| 1 | Painting | c. 1630 | +117 years | Rembrandt etches portraits, scenes and landscapes beside his painting and makes the etching a painter's medium. | [`baroque-painting`](./baroque-painting/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`etching-print`](./etching-print/SKILL.md), [`baroque-painting`](./baroque-painting/SKILL.md).

- Coat the plate with a ground of wax, resin and asphalt and draw through it with a needle of 0.2–0.5 mm.
- Bite the plate in acid in stages; stop out the light lines with varnish after 5–10 minutes and bite the dark lines again.
- Ink the plate, wipe the surface with tarlatan, and leave a film of plate tone where a soft dark is needed.
- Print on dampened paper on a roller press, so that the plate leaves a mark round the image.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Etching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etching); [Wikipedia: Urs Graf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urs_Graf).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 1 | Interior | c. 1790 | +4290 years | Shaker joiners show dovetails and tenons on plain chests and chairs. | [`shaker-interior`](./shaker-interior/SKILL.md) |

| 4 | Interior | 1900 | +4400 years | Gustav Stickley sells Craftsman furniture with through-tenons that show on the face. | [`arts-and-crafts-interior`](./arts-and-crafts-interior/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`shaker-interior`](./shaker-interior/SKILL.md), [`arts-and-crafts-interior`](./arts-and-crafts-interior/SKILL.md), [`arts-and-crafts-architecture`](./arts-and-crafts-architecture/SKILL.md), [`critical-regionalism-architecture`](./critical-regionalism-architecture/SKILL.md), [`japandi-interior`](./japandi-interior/SKILL.md), [`japanese-modern-craft-product`](./japanese-modern-craft-product/SKILL.md), [`contemporary-craft-product`](./contemporary-craft-product/SKILL.md), [`shaker-object`](./shaker-object/SKILL.md), [`arts-and-crafts-object`](./arts-and-crafts-object/SKILL.md).

- Cut the joint in the main wood: a through-tenon of 6 to 12mm shows beyond the face.
- Peg the joint with a wooden pin of 8 to 12mm; do not hide it with filler or paint.
- Use one joint type in a room or a building; repeat it at each corner.

Sources: [Wikipedia, Mortise and tenon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mortise_and_tenon); [Wikipedia, Gamble House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gamble_House_(Pasadena,_California)).

### Exposed services

Ducts, pipes, stairs and cables stay in view on the outside or the ceiling, often colour-coded, so the building shows how it works.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Architecture | 1977 | — | Paris, France: The Centre Pompidou of Renzo Piano and Richard Rogers puts its ducts, pipes and escalators on the outside. | |
| 1 | Interior | c. 1990 | +13 years | Loft conversions leave ducts, pipes and cable trays bare on the ceiling. | [`industrial-loft-interior`](./industrial-loft-interior/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`high-tech-architecture`](./high-tech-architecture/SKILL.md), [`industrial-loft-interior`](./industrial-loft-interior/SKILL.md).

- Leave each duct, pipe and cable tray in view; paint them or leave them raw metal.
- Give each system one colour: for example blue for air, green for water, yellow for electricity.
- Keep the floor plate free; put the services on the facade or the ceiling.

Sources: Kenneth Powell, Richard Rogers: Complete Works (1999).

### Feature amplification

A method that changes a photograph to raise the response of one network layer, so the network patterns grow out of the image.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Internet | 2015 | — | Google: Alexander Mordvintsev and colleagues post the method and its first images in a Google research blog in June 2015. | |
| 1 | Internet | 2015 | +0 years | Google releases the code in July 2015; users post dog-face and eye-filled photographs on web forums and image boards. | [`deepdream-psychedelia`](./deepdream-psychedelia/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`deepdream-psychedelia`](./deepdream-psychedelia/SKILL.md).

- Pick one layer of a trained image network; a middle layer gives eyes and fur, a high layer gives whole animals.
- Change the pixels of the image by small steps so that the layer response rises.
- Repeat at 4 to 8 image sizes (octaves) and mix the results back into one image.
- Feed the output back in and zoom 3% for each frame to make an endless loop.

Sources: [Google Research Blog: Inceptionism (2015)](https://research.google/blog/inceptionism-going-deeper-into-neural-networks/); [Wikipedia: DeepDream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeepDream).

### Feed grid planning

The practice of planning the order of posts so that the profile grid reads as one board.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Internet | c. 2016 | — | web: Scheduling apps add a grid preview of the Instagram profile. | |
| 1 | Internet | c. 2016 | +0 years | Brand and creator profiles plan the grid as one board with a repeat rhythm. | [`cohesive-feed-grid`](./cohesive-feed-grid/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`cohesive-feed-grid`](./cohesive-feed-grid/SKILL.md).

- Preview the next 9 posts in a 3-column grid before posting.
- Fix one preset, one palette and one crop ratio.
- Repeat a rhythm of image types in rows of 3.
- Check the grid after each post.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Instagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instagram).

### Figured silk weaving

The weaving of patterns into silk cloth on a draw loom, so that flowers and ribbons appear in the weave of the cloth.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Fashion | c. 1536 | — | Lyon, France: The silk trade of Lyon grows from the royal privilege that Francis I gives to the city in 1536. | |
| 1 | Fashion | c. 1730 | +194 years | Lyon mills weave light flower brocades for the Rococo gowns of the French court. | [`rococo-court-dress`](./rococo-court-dress/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`rococo-court-dress`](./rococo-court-dress/SKILL.md).

- Set up the loom with a pattern of cords that lift the warp threads in a set order.
- Weave the ground in satin or taffeta, and float the coloured or gold weft to make the pattern.
- Press the cloth and cut it with the pattern in scale with the gown.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Brocade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brocade); [Wikipedia: Canut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canut).

### Fixed-pitch grid

Every character takes one cell of the same width, so text forms columns without a separate grid and a position is a count of characters.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Typeface | 1874 | — | Ilion, New York: The Sholes and Glidden typewriter by E. Remington and Sons moves the carriage one fixed step for each key, so its type must be monospaced. | |
| 1 | Typeface | 1874 | +0 years | Typewriter faces, then Courier for IBM in 1955. | [`monospace`](./monospace/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Internet | 1978 | +104 years | Video terminals such as the DEC VT100 draw the whole screen as a grid of 80 by 24 character cells. | [`terminal-tui-web`](./terminal-tui-web/SKILL.md), [`dark-devtool-ui`](./dark-devtool-ui/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Internet | c. 2014 | +140 years | Brutalist websites set all text in a system monospace face on default browser styles. | [`brutalist-web`](./brutalist-web/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`monospace`](./monospace/SKILL.md), [`terminal-tui-web`](./terminal-tui-web/SKILL.md), [`dark-devtool-ui`](./dark-devtool-ui/SKILL.md), [`brutalist-web`](./brutalist-web/SKILL.md).

- Give every glyph one advance width, 0.60 em in most fonts, or 10 or 12 characters an inch on a typewriter.
- Align columns with spaces and set tables, code and forms on the cell grid.
- On a screen, size the layout in cells: 80 columns by 24 rows is the classic terminal.

Sources: [Monospaced font (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monospaced_font); [Sholes and Glidden typewriter (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sholes_and_Glidden_typewriter); [VT100 (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VT100).

### Flourless reduction sauce

A sauce made from the cooking juices and a stock, reduced and finished with a little cream, butter, fromage blanc or a vegetable purée, with no flour roux.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Cuisine | c. 1962 | — | Roanne, France: The Troisgros brothers made salmon with a sorrel sauce that the cook did not thicken with starch. | |
| 1 | Cuisine | c. 1975 | +13 years | Michel Guérard cut the butter and the cream further and bound his sauces with fromage blanc and vegetable purée. | [`cuisine-minceur`](./cuisine-minceur/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`nouvelle-cuisine`](./nouvelle-cuisine/SKILL.md), [`cuisine-minceur`](./cuisine-minceur/SKILL.md).

- Deglaze the pan with wine or stock, and reduce it by two thirds.
- Add a spoon of cream, a cube of cold butter, or fromage blanc, and whisk it off the heat.
- Season with lemon, vinegar or herbs at the end, and serve 30 to 50 ml of sauce under the food.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Pierre Troisgros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Troisgros); [Wikipedia: Cuisine minceur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_minceur).

### Fully fashioned knitting

Used by: [`quiet-luxury-fashion`](./quiet-luxury-fashion/SKILL.md).

- Knit each panel to its final shape, with the shaping marked by decreases on the edge.
- Join the panels by linking stitch to stitch on a linking machine or by hand.
- Wash and block the piece so that the seams lie flat.

### Garment dyeing

A garment is dyed after it is sewn, in a drum. Cloth, thread and tape take one colour and each piece differs a little.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Fashion | c. 1980 | — | Italy: Massimo Osti dyes made-up garments in a drum for his labels. | |

Used by: [`techwear`](./techwear/SKILL.md).

- Sew the garment from undyed cloth and thread that takes dye.
- Tumble the garment in a drum of dye and water.
- Rinse, dry and finish the garment, and check the colour.

### Garment pleating

A way to pleat cloth after the garment is cut and sewn, with a heat press, so that the pleats stay for good.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Used by: [`japanese-avant-garde-fashion`](./japanese-avant-garde-fashion/SKILL.md).

### Glaze splashing

Lead glazes of different colours are brushed, dripped or splashed on a fired body. They run together in the second firing and make streaks of amber, green and cream.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 700 | — | Henan and Shaanxi, Tang China: Tang kilns make sancai tomb figures and vessels with running lead glazes in amber, green and cream. | |

Used by: [`tang-sancai-figures`](./tang-sancai-figures/SKILL.md).

- Fire the pressed body to about 1050 °C.
- Brush or drip the coloured lead glazes on the biscuit.
- Leave the base bare of glaze.

Used by: [`babylonian-glazed-brick`](./babylonian-glazed-brick/SKILL.md).

- Dry the brick and fire it once, then paint the glaze in colour fields.

### Glitch

A fault in a signal, kept as an image: torn lines, split colour channels and repeated frames.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Film & Video | 1963 | — | Wuppertal: Nam June Paik bends the picture of television sets with magnets in his first Exposition of Music-Electronic Television. | |
| 1 | Internet | c. 2010 | +47 years | Rosa Menkman writes the Glitch Studies Manifesto; designers copy channel splits and torn bands in posters and sites. | [`cyberpunk-web`](./cyberpunk-web/SKILL.md), [`vaporwave`](./vaporwave/SKILL.md), [`glitch-gif`](./glitch-gif/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Film & Video | c. 2015 | +52 years | Short films and music videos shot on old camcorders keep VHS tracking errors and colour bleed. | [`camcorder-revival`](./camcorder-revival/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Internet | 2019 | +56 years | Glitchcore edits split colour channels, repeat frames and add datamosh on fast cuts. | [`glitchcore`](./glitchcore/SKILL.md) |
| 4 | Internet | c. 2020 | +57 years | Cybercore pages add scan lines, split colour channels and torn frames as decoration. | [`cybercore-web`](./cybercore-web/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`cyberpunk-web`](./cyberpunk-web/SKILL.md), [`vaporwave`](./vaporwave/SKILL.md), [`camcorder-revival`](./camcorder-revival/SKILL.md), [`cybercore-web`](./cybercore-web/SKILL.md), [`glitchcore`](./glitchcore/SKILL.md), [`glitch-gif`](./glitch-gif/SKILL.md).

- Shift the red and blue channels by 2 to 8px in opposite directions.
- Cut bands of 4 to 40px and move them sideways by 10 to 60px.
- Run the effect in bursts of 2 to 6 frames at 24 fps, and leave the image clean between bursts.
- Do not apply it to body text.

Sources: [Wikipedia, Glitch art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glitch_art); [Wikipedia, Nam June Paik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nam_June_Paik).

### Goodyear welting

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### Grid transfer

A grid laid over a subject or a photograph and a matching grid on the canvas; the painter copies the picture square by square.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Painting | 1435 | — | Florence: Leon Battista Alberti describes a veil of threads that gives a grid over the scene in De pictura. | |
| 1 | Painting | 1525 | +90 years | Albrecht Dürer prints draughtsmen who draw through a frame with a grid in Underweysung der Messung. |  |
| 2 | Painting | 1967 | +532 years | Chuck Close works from a gridded photograph in Big Self-Portrait. | [`photorealism`](./photorealism/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`photorealism`](./photorealism/SKILL.md).

- Draw a grid of 1 to 5 cm over the reference and a grid of the same ratio on the canvas.
- Copy the contour of each square, one at a time, so that the whole picture stays in the right place.
- Paint each square at the same focus, with an even grain over the full surface.
- Scale up by a fixed ratio, for example 10:1.

Sources: Leon Battista Alberti, De pictura (1435); Albrecht Dürer, Underweysung der Messung (1525).

### Halftone

Tone printed as dots of different size or spacing; at a distance the dots merge into grey or colour.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Internet | 1879 | — | New York: Benjamin Day patents a mechanical tint of dots for printers; photographic halftone screens follow in the 1880s. | |

| 3 | Print | 1962 | +83 years | Andy Warhol has photographs made into halftone screens and pulls them over hand-cut flat colour. | [`pop-screen-print`](./pop-screen-print/SKILL.md) |

- Set the screen at 10 to 30 lines per cm for a print grain, or enlarge the dots to 4 to 8mm for a Pop effect.

### Hat blocking

A milliner steams a felt or straw hat body and pulls it over a carved wooden block, so that it dries in the shape of the crown and the brim.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Fashion | c. 1800 | — | England: Hatters block felt on carved wood blocks long before the Victorian trade; the method scales up with the many hat shapes of the 19th century. | |

Used by: [`victorian-millinery`](./victorian-millinery/SKILL.md).

- Soften the hat body with steam or hot water.
- Pull the body down over the block and tie it at the crown line with a cord.
- Dry it on the block, then trim the brim edge and sew in the lining.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Millinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millinery); [Wikipedia: Felt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felt).

### Hollow terracotta block

A building block of clay that has webs inside. A maker presses it in a mould, so that it dries and fires without a crack and weighs little.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1850 | — | England: English makers in Lambeth and Staffordshire start to sell pressed terracotta blocks for buildings. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1860 | +10 years | Makers in London and Leeds press hollow terracotta blocks for museums, schools and town halls. | [`victorian-architectural-terracotta`](./victorian-architectural-terracotta/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Ceramics | c. 1885 | +35 years | Chicago works make thin, hollow terracotta tile that hangs on the steel frame of a tall building. | [`chicago-school-terracotta`](./chicago-school-terracotta/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`victorian-architectural-terracotta`](./victorian-architectural-terracotta/SKILL.md), [`chicago-school-terracotta`](./chicago-school-terracotta/SKILL.md).

- Press the clay in a plaster mould to the shape and the relief of the block.
- Leave the back open and add webs inside, with a wall of 25 to 50 mm.
- Dry the block slowly, then fire it hard at about 1100 °C.
- Bed the block in mortar and tie it to the backing wall or the steel frame.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Architectural terracotta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architectural_terracotta).

### Inverted firing

A bowl is fired upside down on its rim in a ring of fireclay, so that the wall stays thin and true. The rim has no glaze and takes a metal band.

### Joined-hemisphere throwing

Two large bowls are thrown on the wheel and joined at the rim when leather-hard. The seam is smoothed, and the sphere closes at the top with a collar.

### Lacto-fermentation

Vegetables in a salt brine are soured by lactic bacteria; the acid and the savoury taste keep the food and add depth to a dish.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Cuisine | c. 200 BCE | — | China: Salted and soured vegetables were stored in China in the last centuries BCE; the method spread over Asia and Europe. | |
| 1 | Cuisine | 2004 | +2204 years | The cooks of the Nordic kitchen, led by Noma, made ferments of vegetables, fruit and grain a part of the menu. | [`new-nordic`](./new-nordic/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Cuisine | 2021 | +2221 years | The plant-based menu of Eleven Madison Park used ferments in place of meat and dairy for depth of taste. | [`plant-based-fine-dining`](./plant-based-fine-dining/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`new-nordic`](./new-nordic/SKILL.md), [`plant-based-fine-dining`](./plant-based-fine-dining/SKILL.md).

- Weigh the vegetables and add 2 per cent salt by weight.
- Press the vegetables under their own brine in a closed jar.
- Keep the jar at 18 to 22 °C for 3 to 14 days and taste each day.
- Chill the ferment when the acid is right, and use the juice as a sauce or a seasoning.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Lactic acid fermentation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lactic_acid_fermentation); [Wikipedia: Daniel Humm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Humm).

Used by: [`abbasid-lustreware`](./abbasid-lustreware/SKILL.md), [`hispano-moresque-lustreware`](./hispano-moresque-lustreware/SKILL.md).

- Fire the pot with a tin glaze and let it cool.
- Paint the pattern with a mix of copper or silver salts, ochre and vinegar.
- Fire the pot again at about 600 °C in a smoky kiln.
- Wash off the ochre to show the metallic film.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Lustreware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lustreware); [Wikipedia: Hispano-Moresque ware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispano-Moresque_ware).

### Market menu

The chef writes the menu after the visit to the market or the farm, and the dishes change each day or each week with what is fresh.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Cuisine | c. 1965 | — | Collonges-au-Mont-d'Or, France: Paul Bocuse and the chefs of nouvelle cuisine bought produce at the market and built the menu from it. | |
| 1 | Cuisine | 1971 | +6 years | Alice Waters served one set menu at Chez Panisse that changed each day with the produce of local farms. | [`california-cuisine`](./california-cuisine/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Cuisine | 1992 | +27 years | Yves Camdeborde wrote a fixed menu on a blackboard at La Régalade and changed it with the market. | [`bistronomie`](./bistronomie/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`nouvelle-cuisine`](./nouvelle-cuisine/SKILL.md), [`california-cuisine`](./california-cuisine/SKILL.md), [`bistronomie`](./bistronomie/SKILL.md).

- Buy the produce on the same day, from the same traders or farms.
- Write the menu after the purchase, and keep it short.
- Print or write the menu by hand on the day, and name the farm or the market.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Paul Bocuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Bocuse); [Wikipedia: Chez Panisse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chez_Panisse).

### Matte fade

A grade that lifts the black point to a grey and lowers contrast, so that the photo looks soft and printed.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Photography | c. 1990 | — | United States: Expired and cross-processed film gives lifted blacks and tinted shadows in zine photos of the 1990s. | |
| 1 | Internet | c. 2012 | +22 years | Tumblr blogs reblog faded, pastel-tinted photos with lifted blacks and grain. | [`tumblr-soft-grunge`](./tumblr-soft-grunge/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Internet | 2013 | +23 years | VSCO presets bring the matte lifted-black grade to phone photos. | [`vsco-preset-look`](./vsco-preset-look/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`tumblr-soft-grunge`](./tumblr-soft-grunge/SKILL.md), [`vsco-preset-look`](./vsco-preset-look/SKILL.md).

- Raise the black point of the tone curve to 8% to 15% grey.
- Lower contrast by 10% to 20% and saturation by 20% to 40%.
- Tint the shadows toward pink, green or teal by a small amount.
- Add a grain of 4% to 12%.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Soft grunge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soft_grunge); [Wikipedia: VSCO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VSCO).

### Mesh gradient

A colour field built from many points that each hold a colour, so the colours blend in soft, organic shapes.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Internet | 1998 | — | United States: Adobe Illustrator 8 adds the gradient mesh tool. | |
| 1 | Internet | 2018 | +20 years | Product sites blur large colour blobs into soft aurora fields behind the hero. | [`aurora-gradient-ui`](./aurora-gradient-ui/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Internet | 2019 | +21 years | Hyperpop covers use soft pastel mesh gradients as the ground for chrome objects and stickers. | [`hyperpop-visual`](./hyperpop-visual/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Internet | 2021 | +23 years | Ethereal pages fade pale pastel fields into each other behind thin type. | [`ethereal-web`](./ethereal-web/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`aurora-gradient-ui`](./aurora-gradient-ui/SKILL.md), [`ethereal-web`](./ethereal-web/SKILL.md), [`hyperpop-visual`](./hyperpop-visual/SKILL.md).

- Use 3 to 5 colours of near value; keep the step between them small.
- Blur the field by 80 to 200 px, so that no edge shows.
- Put the field behind the content and give the text a solid or near-solid surface.

Sources: Adobe Illustrator 8 user guide (1998).

### Mezzotint

An intaglio tone process: a rocker roughens the whole plate to a black, and the engraver scrapes and burnishes the lights.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Print | 1642 | — | Amsterdam: Ludwig von Siegen makes a portrait of Amalia Elisabeth, Landgravine of Hesse-Kassel in the new tone process. | |
| 1 | Print | c. 1770 | +128 years | London engravers such as John Raphael Smith and Valentine Green reproduce the portraits of Reynolds and Gainsborough, and Europe calls it the English manner. | [`mezzotint-print`](./mezzotint-print/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`mezzotint-print`](./mezzotint-print/SKILL.md).

- Rock the plate in 20–40 directions with a curved, toothed rocker until it prints a full, even black.
- Scrape and burnish the grain flat where the image needs light; a full burnish prints white.
- Work from dark to light, and check the tone with proofs at each stage.
- Print only 50–100 good impressions; the soft grain wears quickly.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Mezzotint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mezzotint); [Wikipedia: Ludwig von Siegen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwig_von_Siegen).

### Modular figure assembly

A figure is made in many standard parts, such as heads, torsos, arms and props, and the parts are joined and finished by hand. One set of moulds gives many different figures.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 221 BCE | — | Lintong, Shaanxi, China: The state workshops of the First Emperor make heads and hands in moulds and join them to hand-built bodies. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 221 BCE | +0 years | Workshops build the torso by coils, press the head in a two-piece mould and finish each face by hand. | [`qin-terracotta-warriors`](./qin-terracotta-warriors/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Ceramics | 1731 | +1952 years | Meissen modellers cast figures in many small piece moulds and join arms, heads and props with slip. | [`rococo-porcelain-figures`](./rococo-porcelain-figures/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Ceramics | c. 1751 | +1972 years | Vincennes and Sèvres cast biscuit figures in piece moulds, and cut the seams until they disappear. | [`biscuit-porcelain-figures`](./biscuit-porcelain-figures/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`biscuit-porcelain-figures`](./biscuit-porcelain-figures/SKILL.md), [`qin-terracotta-warriors`](./qin-terracotta-warriors/SKILL.md), [`rococo-porcelain-figures`](./rococo-porcelain-figures/SKILL.md).

- Model the figure and cut it into parts.
- Press or cast each part in its own mould.
- Join the parts with slip while the clay is soft.
- Finish the joins and the face by hand, so that each figure differs.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Terracotta Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terracotta_Army); [Wikipedia: Biscuit porcelain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biscuit_porcelain).

### Modular grid

A page or screen divided into equal columns and rows; each element fits a cell or a block of cells, so that the whole layout repeats one measure.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Internet | c. 1958 | — | Zurich: Josef Mueller-Brockmann and the journal Neue Grafik (1958) spread the modular grid for posters and print. | |

| 2 | Logo | c. 1962 | +4 years | Otl Aicher builds the corporate design of Lufthansa on a fixed grid of marks, type and colour. | [`swiss-modernist-mark`](./swiss-modernist-mark/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Internet | 1970 | +12 years | Unimark issues the New York City Transit Graphics Standards Manual, with sign grids for the whole system. | [`swiss-international`](./swiss-international/SKILL.md) |
| 4 | Internet | 2010 | +52 years | Windows Phone 7 builds its start screen from square and wide live tiles on one grid. | [`metro-ui`](./metro-ui/SKILL.md) |
| 5 | Internet | 2012 | +54 years | Bootstrap 2.0 ships a 12 column fluid grid for web pages. | [`bento-grid-ui`](./bento-grid-ui/SKILL.md), [`minimalism-web`](./minimalism-web/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`swiss-international`](./swiss-international/SKILL.md), [`swiss-modernist-mark`](./swiss-modernist-mark/SKILL.md), [`bento-grid-ui`](./bento-grid-ui/SKILL.md), [`minimalism-web`](./minimalism-web/SKILL.md), [`metro-ui`](./metro-ui/SKILL.md), [`rams-functionalism-ui`](./rams-functionalism-ui/SKILL.md), [`ulm-braun-functionalism`](./ulm-braun-functionalism/SKILL.md).

- Divide the page into 4 to 12 equal columns with a gutter of 1/10 to 1/5 of a column width.
- Cut the page into rows of the same module height; align each image and text block to the lines.
- Set text on a baseline grid of one line height, and use 1 to 2 type sizes for each level.
- Scale an image by a whole number of modules.

Sources: Josef Mueller-Brockmann, Grid Systems in Graphic Design (Niggli, 1981).

### Mother sauce system

A system that derives all the sauces of a kitchen from a few base sauces. A cook who knows the base can make many small sauces from it.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Cuisine | 1833 | — | Paris: Carême set the grand sauces (espagnole, velouté, allemande and béchamel) as the base of his sauce chapters. | |
| 1 | Cuisine | 1903 | +70 years | Escoffier cut the grand sauces to five mother sauces in Le Guide culinaire. | [`cuisine-classique`](./cuisine-classique/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`grande-cuisine`](./grande-cuisine/SKILL.md), [`cuisine-classique`](./cuisine-classique/SKILL.md).

- Make each base sauce from a stock and a roux: espagnole, velouté and béchamel.
- Add one item to a base sauce, such as herbs, wine or cream, to make a small sauce.
- Keep the base sauces ready each day, and finish each small sauce to order.

Sources: [Wikipedia: French mother sauces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_sauces); [Wikipedia: Marie-Antoine Carême](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie-Antoine_Car%C3%AAme).

### Motion mask loop

A process that freezes a video except for one masked region, which repeats in a seamless loop.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Internet | 2011 | — | New York: Jamie Beck and Kevin Burg publish the first cinemagraphs and use the name. | |
| 1 | Internet | 2011 | +0 years | The pair shows cinemagraphs of fashion week on Tumblr; brands and photographers copy the form. | [`cinemagraph`](./cinemagraph/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`cinemagraph`](./cinemagraph/SKILL.md).

- Shoot on a tripod so that the still region does not drift by more than 1px.
- Mask the moving region with a soft edge of 4px to 12px.
- Join the loop with a cross-fade of 4 to 8 frames at the loop point.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Cinemagraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinemagraph).

### Optical mixing

Small dots or strokes of pure colour placed side by side, so that the eye mixes them at a distance.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Painting | 1886 | — | Paris, France: Georges Seurat shows A Sunday on La Grande Jatte at the last Impressionist exhibition. | |
| 1 | Photography | 1907 | +21 years | The Autochrome plate of the Lumière brothers builds colour from dyed starch grains, and pictorialists use it for soft colour pictures. | [`pictorialism`](./pictorialism/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`pointillism`](./pointillism/SKILL.md), [`pictorialism`](./pictorialism/SKILL.md).

- Put dots of pure colour side by side; do not mix them on the palette.
- Set complementary colours next to each other in the shadows.
- Keep each dot under 0.5% of the picture width.

Sources: Michel-Eugène Chevreul, The Principles of Harmony and Contrast of Colours (1839).

### Overglaze enamelling

Coloured glass enamels are painted on a glazed and fired piece. A third firing at a low heat fixes them on the glaze.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1465 | — | Jingdezhen, Jiangxi, China: Jingdezhen potters add the first on-glaze enamel colours to blue-and-white porcelain. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1647 | +182 years | Arita potters of the Kakiemon workshop fix a clear red and soft green on a milk-white body. | [`kakiemon-imari-export`](./kakiemon-imari-export/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Ceramics | c. 1650 | +185 years | Kyoto potters paint enamel and gold on a pale crackle glaze, as Ninsei did at the Omuro kiln. | [`kyo-yaki-overglaze`](./kyo-yaki-overglaze/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Ceramics | c. 1720 | +255 years | Johann Gregorius Höroldt arrives at Meissen and builds a palette of enamel colours for the hard-paste porcelain. | [`meissen-baroque-porcelain`](./meissen-baroque-porcelain/SKILL.md) |
| 4 | Ceramics | c. 1745 | +280 years | English porcelain factories paint flowers and insects in enamel on soft-paste tea ware. | [`georgian-porcelain-tea-service`](./georgian-porcelain-tea-service/SKILL.md) |
| 5 | Ceramics | 1923 | +458 years | Painters of the State Porcelain Factory fix flat planes of black and red enamel on white cups. | [`suprematist-porcelain`](./suprematist-porcelain/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`kakiemon-imari-export`](./kakiemon-imari-export/SKILL.md), [`meissen-baroque-porcelain`](./meissen-baroque-porcelain/SKILL.md), [`kyo-yaki-overglaze`](./kyo-yaki-overglaze/SKILL.md), [`georgian-porcelain-tea-service`](./georgian-porcelain-tea-service/SKILL.md), [`suprematist-porcelain`](./suprematist-porcelain/SKILL.md).

- Fire the piece with a clear glaze.
- Paint the design with enamels of red, green, yellow and blue.
- Fire it again in a small muffle kiln at 750 to 850 °C.
- Add gold in a last low firing, when the design needs it.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Overglaze decoration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overglaze_decoration); [Wikipedia: Kakiemon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kakiemon).

### Overhead hands shot

A fixed camera above a table that shows only a pair of hands and an object, so the viewer sees each step of an action.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Internet | c. 2006 | — | YouTube: Gadget fans film the opening of a new device from above and post the clip, and the plain framing becomes the norm of the unboxing video. | |
| 1 | Internet | c. 2006 | +0 years | Gadget fans post top-down clips of new devices on video sites. | [`unboxing-video`](./unboxing-video/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Internet | c. 2010 | +4 years | Close-mic channels use the same fixed top-down frame for tapping and sorting objects. | [`asmr-video`](./asmr-video/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`unboxing-video`](./unboxing-video/SKILL.md), [`asmr-video`](./asmr-video/SKILL.md).

- Fix the camera at 90° above a plain table.
- Frame the object at 40% to 60% of the picture height.
- Show hands only; keep the face out of the frame.
- Hold each step for 4 to 12 seconds.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Unboxing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unboxing).

### Perspective grid

Lines that run to one vanishing point on a horizon, so that a flat surface reads as a floor in deep space.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Painting | c. 1415 | — | Florence: Filippo Brunelleschi demonstrates linear perspective; Alberti writes the method down in De pictura (1435). | |
| 1 | Film & Video | 1968 | +553 years | Stanley Kubrick sets corridors and rooms on one central vanishing point in 2001: A Space Odyssey; Wes Anderson later builds whole films on the flat, centred frame. | [`planimetric-symmetry`](./planimetric-symmetry/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Internet | c. 1980 | +565 years | Cassette Futurism screens and sleeves draw wireframe floors to a horizon. | [`cassette-futurism`](./cassette-futurism/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Film & Video | 1982 | +567 years | Tron draws vector grids that run to a horizon. |  |
| 4 | Internet | 2012 | +597 years | Vaporwave draws neon grid floors under a sunset. | [`vaporwave`](./vaporwave/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`renaissance-painting`](./renaissance-painting/SKILL.md), [`cassette-futurism`](./cassette-futurism/SKILL.md), [`vaporwave`](./vaporwave/SKILL.md), [`planimetric-symmetry`](./planimetric-symmetry/SKILL.md).

- Put the horizon at 50% to 65% of the height and draw 12 to 24 lines to one vanishing point.
- Space the cross lines so that each gap is about 0.8 of the gap in front of it.

Sources: Leon Battista Alberti, De pictura (1435).

- Apply the set to a photo at a strength from 0% to 100%.
- Show the set as a thumbnail with a short name.
- Keep one set for all photos when a feed must match.

A picture or a letter built from square cells that are each on or off, or one flat colour, with no curve and no smooth edge.

Used by: [`pixel-icon`](./pixel-icon/SKILL.md), [`pixel-art-web`](./pixel-art-web/SKILL.md), [`japanese-dense-web`](./japanese-dense-web/SKILL.md), [`eight-bit-tile`](./eight-bit-tile/SKILL.md), [`four-shade-handheld`](./four-shade-handheld/SKILL.md), [`sixteen-bit-sprite`](./sixteen-bit-sprite/SKILL.md), [`isometric-pixel-world`](./isometric-pixel-world/SKILL.md), [`unicode-emoji`](./unicode-emoji/SKILL.md).

- Limit the palette to 1 to 16 colours; do not blend two colours inside one cell.

- Show the image at an integer scale (2x, 3x, 4x) so that no cell blurs.

### Plein air

Work made outside in front of the subject, in the light of the place and the hour.

| 1 | Painting | c. 1880 | +100 years | The Skagen painters and other Nordic painters work on the beach and in the summer night light. | [`nordic-national-romanticism-painting`](./nordic-national-romanticism-painting/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Film & Video | 1945 | +165 years | Rome Open City is shot on real streets in daylight, with little added light. | [`italian-neorealism`](./italian-neorealism/SKILL.md) |

### Pounce transfer

A drawing on paper is pricked along its lines and dabbed with a bag of charcoal dust, so that dots of the design go onto the surface below.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 2 | Ceramics | c. 1640 | +240 years | Delft painters pounce the same pattern onto many plates and tiles with a pricked sheet, the spons. | [`delftware`](./delftware/SKILL.md) |

- Draw the design at full size on paper.
- Prick holes along each line, 2 to 3 mm apart.

### Pre-rendered background

Render a room or a street offline in 3D software, then show it as a fixed image behind a real-time figure or a cursor.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Video game | 1993 | — | Spokane, Washington (the Cyan studio in 1993): Cyan's Myst shows its island as a set of pre-rendered still views. | |

Used by: [`pre-rendered-backgrounds`](./pre-rendered-backgrounds/SKILL.md).

- Fix the camera for each view and compose it like a film frame.
- Render the background with offline light: soft shadows and many lamps.
- Paint a depth mask so that the figure can pass behind props.
- Match the figure's light direction to the render.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Pre-rendering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-rendering); [Wikipedia: Myst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myst).

### Press moulding in a bowl mould

Soft clay is pressed into a fired clay mould that carries a design in reverse. The bowl keeps a sharp relief when it comes out of the mould.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 250 BCE | — | Athens, Greece: Potters of Athens make relief bowls in moulds, now called Megarian bowls. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 30 BCE | +220 years | The workshops of Arretium press stamped bowls in fired moulds and coat them with a red slip. | [`roman-terra-sigillata`](./roman-terra-sigillata/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`roman-terra-sigillata`](./roman-terra-sigillata/SKILL.md).

- Stamp or carve the design into a bowl-shaped mould of clay.
- Fire the mould hard.
- Press soft clay into the mould and let it dry a little.
- Lift out the bowl, then turn the rim and the foot on the wheel.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Megarian bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megarian_bowl); [Wikipedia: Arretine ware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arretine_ware).

### Raku firing

A short, hot firing of one small piece. The potter opens the kiln and pulls the glowing piece out with tongs.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1580 | — | Kyoto, Japan: Chōjirō fires hand-built bowls one at a time for Sen no Rikyū and pulls them from the hot kiln. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1580 | +0 years | The Raku workshop fires each tea bowl alone and pulls it hot from the kiln. | [`raku-tea-bowl`](./raku-tea-bowl/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`raku-tea-bowl`](./raku-tea-bowl/SKILL.md).

- Bisque fire the hand-built piece to about 800 °C.
- Dip it in a lead glaze.
- Fire it alone in a small kiln until the glaze melts, at 800 to 1000 °C.
- Pull it out with tongs and let it cool in the air.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Raku ware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raku_ware).

### Reduction firing

A kiln fires with too little air, so that the flames take oxygen from the glaze and the clay. Iron turns green or black, and copper turns red.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 200 | — | Zhejiang, China: Potters at the Yue kilns fire green-glazed stoneware in a kiln with a smoky, low-air flame. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | 1920 | +1720 years | Bernard Leach builds a Japanese climbing kiln at St Ives and fires ash and celadon glazes in reduction at 1280 °C. | [`anglo-oriental-studio-pottery`](./anglo-oriental-studio-pottery/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`anglo-oriental-studio-pottery`](./anglo-oriental-studio-pottery/SKILL.md).

- Close the damper and the air inlets in the high part of the firing.
- Let the flames fill the kiln with carbon monoxide.
- Keep the reduction for 1 to 3 hours at about 1000 to 1300 °C.
- Open the air and cool the kiln slowly.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Kiln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiln); [Wikipedia: Celadon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celadon).

### Regional sourcing

A way to write a menu from the produce of one region and one season. The cook buys from near farms and markets and names the source on the menu.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 1 | Cuisine | c. 1965 | +40 years | Pupils of Point, among them Bocuse and the Troisgros brothers, took the market menu into nouvelle cuisine. | [`nouvelle-cuisine`](./nouvelle-cuisine/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`haute-regionale`](./haute-regionale/SKILL.md), [`nouvelle-cuisine`](./nouvelle-cuisine/SKILL.md).

- Buy each main food from a producer within the region on the day of the meal.
- Cook the classic dish with that food, and keep the sauce simple.
- Pair the dish with a wine of the same region.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Fernand Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fernand_Point).

### Roof light

An opening in the roof that lets daylight fall into a room from above.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Architecture | c. 125 | — | Rome, Italy: The dome of the Pantheon has an open oculus 8.9 m wide; it is the only source of light. | |

Used by: [`nordic-modernism-architecture`](./nordic-modernism-architecture/SKILL.md).

- Make each opening 1% to 5% of the floor area under it.
- Splay the sides of a deep opening, so that the light spreads before it reaches the floor.
- Put the roof light over the place of use: a reading table, a stair or the centre of a hall.

Sources: Richard Weston, Alvar Aalto (1995).

### Roux and stock base

A pair of kitchen bases: a stock made from bones and meat, and a roux of flour and butter that thickens it. Most French sauces of later styles begin here.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Cuisine | 1651 | — | Paris: La Varenne printed the roux as a sauce base in place of bread crumbs, and the stock as the base of a sauce and a soup. | |
| 1 | Cuisine | 1815 | +164 years | Carême built his grand sauces on stocks and a roux. | [`grande-cuisine`](./grande-cuisine/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Cuisine | c. 1884 | +233 years | Escoffier kept the roux and the stock in the system of the hotel kitchen. | [`cuisine-classique`](./cuisine-classique/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Cuisine | c. 1925 | +274 years | The restaurants of the provinces kept the classic stock and roux sauces. | [`haute-regionale`](./haute-regionale/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`cuisine-francoise`](./cuisine-francoise/SKILL.md), [`grande-cuisine`](./grande-cuisine/SKILL.md), [`cuisine-classique`](./cuisine-classique/SKILL.md), [`haute-regionale`](./haute-regionale/SKILL.md).

- Simmer bones, meat and vegetables in water for several hours and strain the stock.
- Cook flour in butter over low heat to the colour that the sauce needs.
- Whisk the stock into the roux and simmer the sauce until it coats a spoon.

Sources: [Wikipedia: François Pierre La Varenne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Pierre_La_Varenne); [Wikipedia: Roux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roux).

### Salt glazing

Rock salt is thrown into a kiln at stoneware heat. The salt vapour reacts with the clay and forms a thin hard glaze with an orange-peel texture.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1400 | — | Rhineland, Germany: Potters of Siegburg and Raeren glaze stoneware with salt in the 15th century. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1400 | +0 years | Rhineland potters glaze jugs and jars with salt from about 1400, so that the pots hold liquids without a lead lining. | [`rhenish-salt-glaze-stoneware`](./rhenish-salt-glaze-stoneware/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Ceramics | c. 1800 | +400 years | American potters throw salt in the kiln to glaze crocks and jugs, with the same method that the Rhenish potters use. | [`american-stoneware-crock`](./american-stoneware-crock/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`rhenish-salt-glaze-stoneware`](./rhenish-salt-glaze-stoneware/SKILL.md), [`american-stoneware-crock`](./american-stoneware-crock/SKILL.md).

- Fire the stoneware to 1200 to 1300 °C.
- Throw 1 to 2 kg of rock salt for each cubic metre through ports in the kiln wall.
- Seal the ports and let the vapour settle for 20 to 30 minutes.
- Keep the wall 4 mm or more thick, so that the pot does not crack in the heat shock.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Salt glaze pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salt_glaze_pottery).

A cropped capture of an interface that keeps the frame of the original, so the text reads as found, not made.

Ink pushed through a fine mesh with a squeegee; a stencil on the mesh blocks the parts that do not print.

| 3 | Internet | c. 1965 | +58 years | Poster studios screen print posters in flat, bright colours for clubs and protests. | [`pop-art-web`](./pop-art-web/SKILL.md) |

- Stretch a silk or polyester mesh of 40–120 threads a centimetre on a frame.
- Make the stencil by hand cut film or by a photo emulsion exposed through a film positive.
- Pull thick, opaque ink across the screen with a rubber squeegee in one even stroke.
- Print each colour from its own screen and let each layer dry before the next.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Screen printing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Screen_printing); [Wikipedia: Andy Warhol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Warhol).

### Serial repetition

One motif or one frame, repeated with small changes in a set of works, so that the viewer reads the change and not the object.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Painting | 1891 | — | Paris: Claude Monet shows a group of Haystacks canvases, each of one motif in a different light. | |
| 1 | Photography | 1928 | +37 years | Karl Blossfeldt's book Urformen der Kunst shows plant forms one by one in a uniform series. | [`new-objectivity`](./new-objectivity/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Ceramics | c. 1952 | +61 years | Kaj Franck repeats a few plain shapes as a modular set, so that the buyer builds a service from parts. | [`scandinavian-modern-tableware`](./scandinavian-modern-tableware/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Painting | 1958 | +67 years | Frank Stella starts his Black Paintings, in which equal stripes repeat the shape of the canvas. | [`minimalism-painting`](./minimalism-painting/SKILL.md) |
| 4 | Photography | c. 1959 | +68 years | Bernd and Hilla Becher begin their typologies of water towers, blast furnaces and houses on one fixed frame. | [`duesseldorf-school`](./duesseldorf-school/SKILL.md), [`new-topographics`](./new-topographics/SKILL.md) |
| 5 | Painting | 1962 | +71 years | Andy Warhol repeats one soup can or one face in a grid of identical canvases. | [`pop-art-painting`](./pop-art-painting/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`impressionism`](./impressionism/SKILL.md), [`minimalism-painting`](./minimalism-painting/SKILL.md), [`duesseldorf-school`](./duesseldorf-school/SKILL.md), [`new-topographics`](./new-topographics/SKILL.md), [`pop-art-painting`](./pop-art-painting/SKILL.md), [`new-objectivity`](./new-objectivity/SKILL.md), [`scandinavian-modern-tableware`](./scandinavian-modern-tableware/SKILL.md).

- Fix the motif, the format and the viewpoint; change one variable only, such as the hour, the colour or the stripe width.
- Make at least 5 works in the set and show them in one row at one height.
- Keep the spacing between works equal to a fixed unit, such as 10 cm or one panel width.
- Remove the hand: use a mechanical edge, a stencil or a plain lens.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Haystacks (Monet)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haystacks_(Monet)); Bernd and Hilla Becher, Typologien industrieller Bauten (1963).

- Set all the dishes of one service on the table at the same time.
- Place the dishes in a symmetric pattern that a plan on paper shows.
- Clear the whole service at once and set the next service in its place.

| 6 | Ceramics | c. 1925 | +180 years | Factories cast deco dancers and gazelles in plaster moulds, and paint each one by hand. | [`art-deco-ceramic-figures`](./art-deco-ceramic-figures/SKILL.md) |
| 7 | Ceramics | 1928 | +183 years | Works cast the basin, the pan and the bath of a set in plaster moulds and spray each with the same coloured glaze. | [`coloured-bathroom-suite`](./coloured-bathroom-suite/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`art-deco-ceramic-figures`](./art-deco-ceramic-figures/SKILL.md), [`coloured-bathroom-suite`](./coloured-bathroom-suite/SKILL.md), [`cornishware`](./cornishware/SKILL.md), [`fireclay-butler-sink`](./fireclay-butler-sink/SKILL.md), [`parian-statuary`](./parian-statuary/SKILL.md), [`staffordshire-figures`](./staffordshire-figures/SKILL.md), [`victorian-sanitary-ware`](./victorian-sanitary-ware/SKILL.md).

- Make a plaster mould in two or more parts.
- Pour slip into the mould and wait 10 to 20 minutes for a wall of 3 to 5 mm.
- Pour out the rest of the slip.
- Open the mould when the clay is leather-hard and fettle the seam.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Slip casting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slip_casting).

### Slip trailing

Liquid clay, called slip, is trailed from a quill or a pipe onto the leather-hard pot in lines and dots. The lines stay raised under the glaze.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1670 | — | Staffordshire, England: Thomas Toft and other Staffordshire potters trail slip pictures on large dishes. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1670 | +0 years | Staffordshire potters trail cream slip pictures and names on red earthenware dishes. | [`english-slipware`](./english-slipware/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`english-slipware`](./english-slipware/SKILL.md).

- Mix slip to the thickness of cream.
- Fill a pipe or a bag with the slip.
- Draw lines 2 to 4 mm wide on a pot in a contrast colour.
- Dip the pot in clear lead glaze and fire it at about 1050 °C.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Slipware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slipware).

### Soak-stain

Thin paint is poured on raw, unprimed canvas that lies on the floor; the cloth drinks the colour and the colour and the weave become one.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Painting | 1952 | — | New York: Helen Frankenthaler pours thinned oil on raw canvas in Mountains and Sea. | |
| 1 | Painting | 1953 | +1 years | Morris Louis and Kenneth Noland visit the studio of Frankenthaler and take up the stain method; Louis turns it into his Veils from 1954. | [`colour-field-painting`](./colour-field-painting/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Painting | c. 1960 | +8 years | Noland moves from oil to thinned acrylic, which stains the cloth with no halo; Louis stains with thinned Magna acrylic from the first Veils in 1954. | [`colour-field-painting`](./colour-field-painting/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`colour-field-painting`](./colour-field-painting/SKILL.md), [`abstract-expressionism`](./abstract-expressionism/SKILL.md).

- Thin oil with turpentine, or acrylic with water, until it flows like ink.
- Lay the canvas flat and unprimed on the floor; pour in pools and guide the paint with a sponge or a tilt of the cloth.
- Leave 30% to 60% of the cloth bare so that the raw colour is part of the picture.
- Work at a size of 2 metres or more so that the field is wider than the viewer's sight.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Helen Frankenthaler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helen_Frankenthaler); Helen Frankenthaler, Mountains and Sea (1952).

### Sous vide

Food is sealed in a bag and cooked in a water bath at an exact low temperature, so that the whole piece reaches the same core heat.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Cuisine | 1974 | — | Roanne, France: Georges Pralus cooked foie gras in a sealed bag for the Troisgros kitchen to lose less fat. | |
| 1 | Cuisine | c. 2000 | +26 years | Chefs of the avant-garde kitchen, such as Heston Blumenthal, took the water bath into the tasting menu. | [`molecular-gastronomy`](./molecular-gastronomy/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Cuisine | 2011 | +37 years | The book Modernist Cuisine gave tables of time and temperature for each food. | [`modernist-cuisine`](./modernist-cuisine/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`nouvelle-cuisine`](./nouvelle-cuisine/SKILL.md), [`molecular-gastronomy`](./molecular-gastronomy/SKILL.md), [`modernist-cuisine`](./modernist-cuisine/SKILL.md).

- Season the food and seal it in a bag with the air removed.
- Set the water bath to the target core temperature, for example 56 °C for beef.
- Cook for the time that the thickness of the piece needs, from 1 to 48 hours.
- Chill or sear the food just before service.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Sous-vide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sous-vide); [Wikipedia: Modernist Cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modernist_Cuisine).

### Speed ramp

Change the speed of one clip inside a single shot, from slow motion to fast play and back.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Film & Video | 2006 | — | United States: Zack Snyder uses ramps between slow and fast motion in the fights of 300. | |
| 1 | Internet | c. 2020 | +14 years | CapCut and similar editors give a curve tool, and editors cut clips to music with ramps. | [`velocity-edit`](./velocity-edit/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`velocity-edit`](./velocity-edit/SKILL.md).

- Set a speed curve with points at 20% and 400%.
- Place the slow point on the strong beat.
- Smooth the curve over 6 to 12 frames.
- Add a 2px zoom or shake on the jump.

Sources: [Wikipedia: 300 (film)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/300_(film)); [Wikipedia: CapCut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CapCut).

### Spherification

A liquid dropped into a calcium bath forms a thin skin of alginate gel; the sphere holds its shape on the spoon and bursts in the mouth.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Cuisine | 2003 | — | Roses, Spain: The elBulli kitchen made the alginate sphere a dish in 2003; the gel came from the food industry. | |
| 1 | Cuisine | 2011 | +8 years | The book Modernist Cuisine set out the recipes and the times, and Alinea served spheres on the tasting menu. | [`modernist-cuisine`](./modernist-cuisine/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`molecular-gastronomy`](./molecular-gastronomy/SKILL.md), [`modernist-cuisine`](./modernist-cuisine/SKILL.md).

- Mix 0.5 per cent sodium alginate into the flavoured liquid and rest it until the bubbles go.
- Drop the liquid with a spoon into a calcium bath of 1 per cent for 1 to 2 minutes.
- Lift the sphere out and rinse it in clean water.
- Serve within 5 minutes, because the gel keeps setting towards the centre.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Spherification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spherification); [Wikipedia: El Bulli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Bulli).

### Sponge stamping

A piece of sea sponge is dipped in ceramic colour and dabbed or stamped on a biscuit pot. The soft print is quick and needs little skill.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1840 | — | Scotland and Staffordshire: Potters sponge colour on cheap earthenware for export to North America. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1840 | +0 years | Scottish and Staffordshire potters dab colour with a cut sponge on cheap earthenware for the home and for export. | [`spongeware`](./spongeware/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`spongeware`](./spongeware/SKILL.md).

- Cut a sponge into a small shape, about 2 to 4 cm wide.
- Dip the sponge in a stain of one colour.
- Dab the sponge on the biscuit in rings or in a loose mottle.
- Leave a band of 1 to 2 cm at the rim clear, then dip the pot in a clear glaze.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Transferware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transferware).

### Sprig moulding

A small relief is pressed in a mould of fired clay, lifted out and fixed to the damp body with water. Potters use it to add figures and leaves.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1740 | — | Staffordshire, England: Staffordshire potters press white clay reliefs into moulds and fix them to the body of salt-glazed stoneware. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | 1775 | +35 years | Wedgwood workers press fine white sprigs of classical figures onto the stained jasper body of urns and vases. | [`jasperware-neoclassical`](./jasperware-neoclassical/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`jasperware-neoclassical`](./jasperware-neoclassical/SKILL.md).

- Carve the relief in reverse in a small mould of fired clay.
- Press a thin piece of soft clay into the mould and trim it flat.
- Lift the sprig out with the tip of a knife.
- Wet the body and press the sprig on it, then fettle the edge.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Sprigging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sprigging); [Wikipedia: Jasperware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasperware).

### Three-point lighting

A key light, a fill light and a back light on one subject, so that the face is modelled and stands out from the ground.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Film & Video | c. 1920 | — | Hollywood, United States: In the 1920s Hollywood cameramen light stars with a key, a fill and a back light; in the 1930s the method is the studio standard. | |
| 1 | Photography | c. 1930 | +10 years | George Hurrell and other studio portraitists bring key, fill and back light to portraits for film and fashion magazines. | [`fashion-editorial`](./fashion-editorial/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Internet | c. 2022 | +102 years | Creators light skin with a soft key at the window, a fill and a ring light, which gives the dewy sheen. | [`clean-girl`](./clean-girl/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`classical-hollywood`](./classical-hollywood/SKILL.md), [`fashion-editorial`](./fashion-editorial/SKILL.md), [`clean-girl`](./clean-girl/SKILL.md).

- Set the key light 30 to 45 degrees off the camera axis and above eye level.
- Set the fill on the other side at 1/4 to 1/2 of the key.
- Put the back light behind the subject, so that a thin rim of light separates hair and shoulders from the ground.

Sources: Kristin Thompson, David Bordwell and Janet Staiger, The Classical Hollywood Cinema (1985).

### Three-stage firing

One firing in three stages turns a slip black and leaves the clay red. Air is let in, shut out, and let in again.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 700 BCE | — | Corinth, Greece: Corinthian potters learn to make a black gloss and a red clay in one firing. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 700 BCE | +0 years | Corinthian and Athenian painters draw black silhouettes in gloss slip, and the three stages fix the black. | [`greek-black-figure`](./greek-black-figure/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Ceramics | c. 530 BCE | +170 years | Athenian painters keep the same firing and paint the background black instead of the figure. | [`greek-red-figure`](./greek-red-figure/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`greek-black-figure`](./greek-black-figure/SKILL.md), [`greek-red-figure`](./greek-red-figure/SKILL.md).

- Fire the pot in an open kiln with air, so that the whole pot turns red.
- Close the vents and add green wood, so that the whole pot turns black.
- Open the vents and let the air in again at a lower heat.
- Take out the pot: the fine slip stays black and the bare clay becomes red.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Black-figure pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-figure_pottery); [Wikipedia: Red-figure pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red-figure_pottery).

### Thrown and assembled form

A potter throws two or more separate parts and joins them at the leather-hard stage. The joined form has a shape that no single throw can make.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1955 | — | London, England: Hans Coper throws parts of a vessel on the wheel and joins them into stems, discs and spades. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1955 | +0 years | Hans Coper joins thrown cylinders, discs and bowls into bottles and cup forms in Lucie Rie's studio. | [`british-modernist-studio`](./british-modernist-studio/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`british-modernist-studio`](./british-modernist-studio/SKILL.md).

- Throw each part separately, a cylinder, a disc or a bowl.
- Let the parts dry to leather-hard.
- Join them with slip and press them together.
- Turn and smooth the join and the foot.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Hans Coper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Coper); [Wikipedia: Lucie Rie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucie_Rie).

### Tie-dye

A way to colour cloth in which parts are tied, folded or bound before the dye bath, so that the bound parts resist the dye.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Fashion | c. 750 | — | Nara, Japan: Cloth with tied resist patterns, known as shibori, survives from Japan of the eighth century. | |
| 1 | Fashion | 1966 | +1216 years | American counterculture makers dye T-shirts and skirts at home, and wear them at concerts and festivals. | [`hippie-bohemian-dress`](./hippie-bohemian-dress/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`hippie-bohemian-dress`](./hippie-bohemian-dress/SKILL.md).

- Fold, twist or gather the wet cloth, and bind it with string or rubber bands.
- Soak the cloth in a dye bath, or apply dye to each section.
- Rinse, untie and dry the cloth; each piece shows a different pattern.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Tie-dye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tie-dye); [Wikipedia: Shibori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shibori).

### Tin glazing

A pot is dipped in a glaze with tin oxide in it. The glaze dries to a white, opaque coat on which a potter paints with oxides before the glaze firing.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 800 | — | Basra, Iraq: Abbasid potters add tin oxide to lead glaze to copy white Chinese porcelain. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1440 | +640 years | Luca della Robbia covers terracotta sculpture with white tin glaze, so that church reliefs stand in the weather. | [`della-robbia-relief`](./della-robbia-relief/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Ceramics | c. 1450 | +650 years | Italian potters paint cobalt labels on the white tin glaze of drug jars made for the shelves of pharmacies. | [`albarello-apothecary-ware`](./albarello-apothecary-ware/SKILL.md) |
| 3 | Ceramics | c. 1500 | +700 years | Urbino painters brush whole scenes on the raw tin glaze before the firing, so that the colours sink into the glaze. | [`istoriato-maiolica`](./istoriato-maiolica/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`albarello-apothecary-ware`](./albarello-apothecary-ware/SKILL.md), [`istoriato-maiolica`](./istoriato-maiolica/SKILL.md), [`della-robbia-relief`](./della-robbia-relief/SKILL.md).

- Fire the pot once to a biscuit at about 950 °C.
- Dip the biscuit in a bath of glaze with 5 to 10 % tin oxide, so that the coat is about 0.5 mm thick.
- Paint on the dry raw glaze with cobalt, copper, manganese or antimony.
- Fire the glaze at about 1000 °C, so that the colours sink into the glaze.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Tin-glazed pottery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tin-glazed_pottery).

### Transfer printing

A design is engraved on a copper plate, printed on thin paper in a ceramic ink and moved from the paper to the glazed or biscuit piece.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Ceramics | c. 1756 | — | Liverpool and Worcester, England: John Sadler and Guy Green of Liverpool print tiles and Worcester soon prints porcelain. | |
| 1 | Ceramics | c. 1756 | +0 years | Soft-paste tea ware takes printed landscapes and flower designs in black and blue. | [`georgian-porcelain-tea-service`](./georgian-porcelain-tea-service/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`georgian-porcelain-tea-service`](./georgian-porcelain-tea-service/SKILL.md).

- Engrave the design on a copper plate.
- Print it on thin tissue paper in ceramic ink.
- Press the wet tissue on the piece and rub it down.
- Wash the paper away and fire the piece to fix the ink.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Transfer printing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transfer_printing); [Wikipedia: Worcester porcelain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Worcester).

### Trompe-l'oeil

Painted perspective that continues the real room, so that a flat wall or ceiling seems to open into space.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Painting | c. 1465 | — | Mantua, Italy: Andrea Mantegna paints an open oculus with figures that look down into the Camera degli Sposi (1465 to 1474). | |
| 1 | Painting | 1532 | +67 years | Giulio Romano paints the Sala dei Giganti in Mantua so that the walls and the vault seem to fall on the viewer. | [`mannerism-painting`](./mannerism-painting/SKILL.md) |
| 2 | Architecture | c. 1685 | +220 years | Andrea Pozzo paints a false dome and an open vault over the nave of Sant'Ignazio in Rome. | [`baroque-architecture`](./baroque-architecture/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`renaissance-painting`](./renaissance-painting/SKILL.md), [`mannerism-painting`](./mannerism-painting/SKILL.md), [`baroque-architecture`](./baroque-architecture/SKILL.md).

- Set the vanishing point at the eye of a viewer who stands at one marked spot in the room.
- Continue the real cornice, columns and arches in paint for 1 to 3 storeys of false height.
- Paint the sky or the open part with the lightest value; keep the painted frame at the value of the real stone.

Sources: Martin Kemp, The Science of Art (1990); Andrea Pozzo, Perspectiva pictorum et architectorum (1693).

### Tweed weaving

A rough woollen twill, woven from yarn that is dyed in the wool. A check or a heather mix that comes from the fleck of colours.

| Step | Domain | Year | Lag | Route | Styles |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Origin | Fashion | c. 1830 | — | Scottish Borders: Mills of the Borders weave the woollen twill, and a London merchant reads the name 'tweel' as 'tweed'. | |
| 1 | Fashion | 1860 | +30 years | Highland estates order district checks in tweed for the keeper and the guest, and tailors cut sporting jackets from them. | [`country-sporting-tweed`](./country-sporting-tweed/SKILL.md) |

Used by: [`country-sporting-tweed`](./country-sporting-tweed/SKILL.md).

- Dye the wool in the fleece in several heather colours and blend the colours in the card.
- Spin a coarse woollen yarn and weave it in a 2/2 twill on a loom.
- Full and finish the cloth in water, so that it shrinks, thickens and sheds rain.

Sources: [Wikipedia: Tweed (cloth)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tweed_(cloth)); [Wikipedia: Harris Tweed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harris_Tweed).

### Underglaze transfer printing
